From the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection titled Jewel Lamp Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate, Clarifying the Suchness of Profound Dependent Origination, this is the commentary on the third chapter, "Holding the Immeasurable Benefits of the Perfection of Wisdom and Honoring the Stupa." ^3-110

###### **Path of Meditation of Belief for the Purpose of Others** ^3-3-2-1-2-6-2-1-1-6-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-111]]

Path of meditation of devoted interest for the sake of others. To teach the third, the path of meditation of devoted interest for the sake of others, it is taught with nine verses starting with "The Victor... to Śakra." The meaning is: "The Victor," the Bhagavan who has conquered the four maras, completely put a "question to Śakra," the lord of gods who possesses the splendor of a hundred ordinary gods, having placed all the realms equal to the sands of the Ganges completely filled with the relics of the Victor without intervening space in one portion, and having placed a volume of the perfection of wisdom in the second portion, asking, "Which do you take?" His answer was: "If they were Buddha realms equal to the number of sands of the river Ganges, and all those realms were completely filled and packed with the relics of the Victor" without intervening space, I, the lord of gods, "would take this very" perfection of wisdom written in a volume. ^3-111

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-112]]

"For what reason" do you take the volume of the perfection of wisdom? "It is not that I," Śakra, "am lacking in respect" and faith "for the relics"—indeed I am extremely respectful—however, the reason for taking the perfection of wisdom is that because all the paths are thoroughly embraced and permeated by such a perfection of wisdom, in dependence on it "these relics of the Victor too have become worthy of all beings paying homage and making offerings." "Just as those who depend on a king," such as his ministers and so forth, "also obtain reverence and respect from others," the relics of the Buddha, endowed with immeasurable qualities, are accomplished in dependence on this very perfection of wisdom. ^3-112

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-113]]

If one thinks, "Well, even if it arose in that way through the power of the perfection of wisdom, since the Buddha has passed into nirvana and is free from acting in that way, the relics are not worthy of offerings," to establish that they are worthy by means of an example: "A precious wish-fulfilling jewel" endowed with all qualities such as granting needs and desires and possessing immeasurable value, "whichever vessel it is kept" or placed "in, such as a casket"—a storage box, amulet box, or so forth—"that vessel is worthy of homage," and "even if" the jewel "is taken out of that vessel," one generates great joy toward "that casket" which previously acted as the vessel in which it was kept, thinking, "Because it is the vessel in which the precious jewel was kept, it is unlike others." "That" the vessel "obtained such qualities is through the power of that precious jewel." ^3-113

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-114]]

"Similarly to that example," through the qualities of "that" supreme "precious perfection of wisdom, even though the Victor has passed into nirvana," the relics, which are like its vessel, too have obtained immeasurable qualities such as being made offerings to by others and clearing away the sins and obscurations of beings. "Therefore," whichever bodhisattva wishes to rejoice in, have devoted interest in, and completely uphold obtaining "the immeasurable qualities of the Victor" for the sake of others, "that one should write, hold," bind, and take up "this perfection of wisdom; for this is the" sole entrance to "liberation" from all bindings of apprehending signs and all obscurations. ^3-114

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-115]]

If one asks, "Why, since there are other perfections such as giving, is only the perfection of wisdom made the principal and posited in the three devoted interests?", to teach the reason for that, it is stated: Since one must practice giving by means of being thoroughly embraced by the wisdom that realizes the non-apprehension of the three spheres—the substance to be given, the giver, and the recipient of the gift and so forth—the phenomenon that "precedes the perfection of giving is wisdom," and "ethics, patience, diligence, and concentration too" must "similarly" be preceded by wisdom. ^3-115

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-116]]

Why is that? If all virtuous phenomena such as giving are bound by apprehending signs and stained by the defilement of inferior mental engagement, they will be wasted by falling into samsara or the extreme of peace; so in order that they are not wasted in that way, dedicating them to unsurpassable complete awakening, they are completely embraced on the path by the perfection of wisdom which completely purifies giving and so forth by means of the wisdom that realizes non-apprehension. Thus, this wisdom "shows exactly one method of the path" in which "all" virtuous phenomena such as giving have one taste as the "cause of a Buddha." "For example, in Jambudvīpa there are many thousands of tens of millions of different trees" such as sandalwood and aloeswood, and though they have various colors, shapes, and scents and so forth and "many forms" with many aspects of color and shape of branches, fruits, leaves, and flowers and so forth, except for "their shadows" merely being expressed as synonyms for shadows of trees, the shadows do not have various differing colors and scents and so forth like the trees, and "shadows do not have distinctions" like the individual trees from their own side. "Similarly to that example, these five perfections" of the Victor, "such as giving," too, if embraced by the wisdom that realizes the non-apprehension of the three spheres, "will attain the very name" of perfections, and that they become supramundane perfections is also by the power of this perfection of wisdom. ^3-116

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-117]]

If the virtues "such as giving" are completely "dedicated" to attaining "omniscience," "all these" six perfections, the causes for the result of all those roots of virtue called unsurpassable complete awakening, will become one taste without distinction, and having spontaneously accomplished the two bodies, the Truth Body and Form Body, their enlightened activities will become equal to space. From the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection titled Jewel Lamp Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate, Clarifying the Suchness of Profound Dependent Origination, this is the commentary on the fourth chapter, "Thoroughly Expressing the Qualities." ^3-117

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-118]]

Knowing that abandoning the faults of the fabricated through these three devoted interests is familiarization, in order to abandon the fabricated, showing the defining characteristics of the fabricated perfection of wisdom by way of positive consequence: If whichever bodhisattva, without themselves perfectly knowing the profound meaning of the Mother, emptiness, exactly as it is, thoroughly shows these five aggregates—form, perception, feeling, volition, and consciousness—as impermanent, suffering, lacking a personal self, and so forth, by way of clinging to them as truly existent, and speaks of and meditates on this saying, "This is the perfection of wisdom," that is practicing the counterfeit perfection of wisdom or the fabricated perfection of wisdom, because it merely shows a path that takes conventional proliferations as the object of its mode of apprehension. The wise who have unmistakably realized the meaning of the natural state always unmistakably show and uphold the meaning of the scriptural phenomena of the pronouncements, such as the sutras of the perfection of wisdom, and do not cause the essence of the teaching to decline or be destroyed. ^3-118

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-119]]

Having thus taught that the counterfeit perfection of wisdom is to be discarded, explaining how to train in the genuine perfection of wisdom by way of negative consequence: "Whatever phenomenon" does not truly exist as "form," one does not cling to it as nonexistent, "and similarly it is not feeling, and one does not apprehend it" as truly existent "as perception, and does not apprehend it as consciousness, and" does not apprehend it as truly existent as "volition" either, but rather perfectly knows "all phenomena" of samsara and nirvana, illustrated by the entities and features of phenomena such as form being impermanent and so forth, in the manner of being "empty by nature of arising," and shows them to others in that way as well; "this is practicing" the genuine perfection of wisdom, "the supreme perfection of wisdom." ^3-119

###### **Benefits of the Path of Meditation of Belief** ^3-3-2-1-2-7-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-120]]

The benefits of the path of meditation of devoted interest. The seventh, the benefits of the path of meditation of devoted interest: The Ornament says, from "On the perfection of wisdom..." up to "...are asserted as reverence and praise." Its defining characteristic is a quality attained by the power of meditating on the path of meditation of devoted interest, which has become the method for attaining it. If divided, there are twenty-seven. Its boundary is from the second moment of meditating on the path of meditation of devoted interest up to the ground of a Buddha. To show that, in order to make the bodhisattva of devoted interest thus enter the higher paths and to generate enthusiasm, the Buddhas offer praise, reverence, commendation, and so forth, the meaning taught by the seven verses from "If some..." and so forth is: Compared to some person establishing or guiding however many hosts of sentient beings are dwelling in realms equal in number to however many grains of sand there are in the river Ganges into the state of Hearer or Solitary Realizer arhats, "if some person writes this perfection of wisdom" in letters "and gives" that volume "to a supreme sentient being," a bodhisattva, "this is much more exceedingly superior" in merit than the former. ^3-120

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-121]]

If one asks, "Why is that?", it is because the former is for the sake of only some sentient beings, whereas the latter becomes for the sake of all sentient beings. For those bodhisattvas, supreme among beings who speak, having trained in this path, "will show all phenomena here" to be "empty by nature," and through having heard, realized, and meditated on that meaning of emptiness, those with dull faculties belonging to the Hearer lineage will quickly "touch" and attain "liberation," completely liberated from samsara; those with medium faculties belonging to the Solitary Realizer lineage, having seen that meaning, "will attain the awakening of a Solitary Realizer;" and "those with sharp faculties" belonging to the Mahayana lineage, having realized all phenomena of samsara and nirvana to be equality, empty of true existence, "will touch the awakening of a Buddha." ^3-121

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-122]]

Showing the example of how results do not arise without causes in that way: Just as, without a sprout as the substantial cause, the arising of a trunk and a tree in the world is entirely nonexistent and impossible, how could branches, leaves, flowers, and fruits arise on that nonexistent trunk? They would not arise. ^3-122

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-123]]

Similarly, if conventional and ultimate awakening mind, which are like the sprout and are the principal meaning taught in the sutras of the Mother, do not exist, the arising of a Victor, who is like the trunk, is entirely nonexistent in the world. If they do not exist, how could Śakra, Brahmā, Hearers, and so forth arise, being equivalent to the branches, leaves, and so forth, which are the results of having practiced virtue and the path after the Buddha's Dharma has been taught? Because there would be no cause. ^3-123

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-124]]

Thus, just as "when" the "sun's disk, having risen," emits limitless nets of "light rays" in the ten directions, "at that time sentient beings" in the world realms "strive at doing" many types of "work", "similarly", if "the wise", in order to attain the resultant perfection of wisdom, the exalted wisdom of omniscient knowledge, generate conventional and ultimate awakening mind in their own mindstreams, then based on that, the unsurpassable exalted wisdom of a Buddha will arise, and based on that exalted wisdom itself acting as the empowering condition, by emitting light rays of the profound and vast Dharma to disciples, sentient beings will come to be endowed with temporary higher status and the ultimate phenomena of the qualities of a Sugata. ^3-124

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-125]]

Showing by way of negative consequence that without awakening mind those results do not exist: "For example, if the" great "nāga lord did not exist in Lake Anavatapta, how could the rivers", such as the Ganges, which arise "by his power, flow in this Jambudvīpa"? If there were no rivers, because there is no moisture, "flowers" such as lotuses and utpalas "and fruits would not arise either". "Also, in the great ocean", "many different forms" of "various jewels" arising from the connection of touching the water "would not exist". ^3-125

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-126]]

"Similarly" to that example, if "in this world" realm "too" there were no scriptural pronouncements and awakening mind with the essence of emptiness and compassion, which are like the nāga lord, "how could" the arising of benefit and "bliss" and the "stream of exalted wisdom" that clears away evil deeds and obscurations, which arise from the power of "the Sugatas, arise in all these worlds"? They would not arise. If there were no such water stream of exalted wisdom, there would be no "increasing" of the qualities of the grounds and paths, such as the perfection of generosity, and if those did not exist, the results, the "three awakenings", would not exist either, and the immeasurable "phenomena of a Buddha", such as the ten powers, which are like an ocean of qualities of abandonment and realization, would not exist either. Therefore, one should know that generating the two types of precious awakening mind, which are the sole root and source of all the qualities of the Mahayana path and result, in one's own mindstream and striving at the methods to ensure they do not decline but rather increase and expand higher and higher is the root of all excellent collections. ^3-126

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-127]]

For example, in this world realm, if "however many" "beings" there are, such as light-emitting stars, lamps, and fireflies, that dispel darkness and illuminate forms, simultaneously "emitted" "lights" in order to illuminate the directions, the arising of the light rays of the "sun's disk" alone would be much more extraordinarily exalted and supreme. All other lights of the multitudes of illuminators, such as stars, "would not amount" even "to a fraction", merely belonging to a portion, of the sun's light. "Similarly", "Hearers" and so forth "would not amount to a fraction" of the "qualities of a bodhisattva". Therefore, one should know that the Buddhas praising bodhisattvas is also factual. This concludes the commentary on the fifth chapter, the Categories of Merit, from the Jewel Lamp Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate, Clarifying the Profound Reality of Dependent Arising, a Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection. ^3-127

###### **Path of Meditation of Rejoicing** ^3-3-2-1-2-8-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-128]]

The path of meditation of rejoicing. Although in the Hundred Thousand and so forth, as well as in the Ornament, the path of meditation of dedication is taught as the eighth, since there is no contradiction in meaning, here the order is reversed from that, and the path of meditation of rejoicing is taught as the eighth. To show that very point, there is one verse from the Ornament: from "By means of skillful methods and non-apprehension..." up to "...is stated here." That Mahayana subsequent clear realization that is endowed with the realization that apprehends the sound and meaning of meditating on joy in the roots of virtue of either oneself or others as suitable to be mixed, is the definition of the path of meditation of rejoicing. If divided, there are two: the paths of meditation of rejoicing that meditate on joy in the roots of virtue of oneself and of others. Its boundaries are from the first ground up to the end of the continuum. Since causing the roots of virtue to be dedicated to unsurpassable awakening to increase further depends on rejoicing, to show first the path of meditation of rejoicing, the meaning taught by the one verse beginning with "The multitudes of Hearers..." is as follows: Even if "the multitudes of Hearers" were to generate, for their own sake, a great multitude of merit thoroughly endowed with "however many" virtues arising from giving "generosity", arising from "ethical discipline", and roots of virtue arising from "meditation", the "multitude of merit" of the multitudes of Hearers "would not amount to a fraction", even one belonging to a mere portion of that, of a bodhisattva generating a single instant of a mind of "rejoicing" that is embraced by the wisdom realizing all roots of virtue of all ordinary and noble beings as non-apprehendable and by the special skillful means of dedicating to unsurpassable awakening. This is because Hearers do not possess such special means and wisdom. ^3-128

###### **Path of Meditation of Dedication** ^3-3-2-1-2-9-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-129]]

The path of meditation of dedication. To show the ninth, the path of meditation of dedication, it is stated in the Ornament: from "Special thorough dedication..." up to "...is the nature of great merit arising." That Mahayana subsequent clear realization that is endowed with the realization that apprehends the sound and meaning of transforming the roots of virtue of either oneself or others into factors of perfect awakening as suitable to be mixed, is the definition of the path of meditation of dedication. If divided, there are twelve: the path of meditation of special thorough dedication, the path of meditation of dedication with the aspect of non-apprehension, the path of meditation of dedication having the defining characteristic of being unmistaken, the path of meditation of isolated dedication, the path of meditation of dedication remembering the nature of the Buddha's multitude of merit, the path of meditation of dedication skillful in means, the path of meditation of signless dedication, the path of meditation of dedication permitted by the Buddhas, the path of meditation of dedication not included in the three realms, the path of meditation of small dedication where great merit arises, the path of meditation of medium dedication of great merit, and the path of meditation of great dedication of great merit. Its boundaries are from the first up to the tenth ground. Since causing those roots of virtue—which were previously increased inexhaustibly by rejoicing—to be directed toward unsurpassable awakening depends on dedication, to show the path of meditation of dedication after the path of meditation of rejoicing, the meaning taught by the eight verses beginning with "Of previous past times..." is as follows: Whichever "many" hundreds of thousands of millions of "previous Buddhas" appeared in the time of "past" eons, and the limitless past Buddhas who dwelled in "many limitless worlds" of thousands of millions, and whichever "protectors of the world" have passed beyond the extremes of existence and peace into non-abiding nirvana, and are now living and remaining, continuing to teach the "precious Dharma" in order to exhaust the suffering of migrating beings—all of them, from first stably generating the mind of supreme awakening in their mindstreams up to finally becoming perfectly awakened and leading immeasurable trainees completely out of cyclic existence, down to the very "waning" or exhaustion of the sublime Dharma of those Victorious Ones—whichever "merit" of their first generating of the mind up to that time, and whichever possessing magnificent roots of virtue of "activities" such as the six perfections and the four means of gathering, and whichever "results" such as the ten powers and the eighteen unshared phenomena of a Buddha were attained, and whichever other contaminated and uncontaminated "virtues" of "the Buddhas' sons", the bodhisattvas, "along with" "the Hearers" and Solitary Realizers, in short, of all individuals who are learners and non-learners—all those, by gathering them into one with one's mind, that bodhisattva "rejoices" with supreme joy from the depths of their heart, without jealousy and so forth. Embracing the intention—the generation of the mind—with the wisdom realizing the three spheres as non-apprehendable, "in order to attain" "great" unsurpassable "awakening" as the cause for accomplishing the welfare of migrating beings equaling the limits of space, one should "dedicate" all those roots of virtue of oneself and others. ^3-129

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-130]]

Having thus taught the Mahayana dedication in general, to show that one must make a special dedication by way of the non-apprehension of the three spheres: Regarding "that" "thorough dedication" "likewise", "if" one has "the perception" clinging to it as true or apprehending it as true, thinking "this is" the dedicating "mind", and one has the perception of "awakening"—"where" it is dedicated—as true, and the perception arises of "sentient beings"—for whose sake the thorough dedication is made—as true, then, since one is bound by the perception or apprehension of signs that manifest adherence to the three—the action, agent, and object—as true, one "abides in a view" with the focal aim of true apprehension; and since "that mind" "has an apprehension" of "attachment" to the three spheres through true adherence, it is "not included in" "thorough dedication" having an unmistaken nature. ^3-130

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-131]]

If one asks, "Then how should one dedicate?" "If" a bodhisattva comes to "know" how "these phenomena"—the virtue to be dedicated, the dedicating mind, and so forth—are inherently ceased and "exhausted" or non-existent in terms of true establishment, and how that unsurpassed awakening "where" it is "thoroughly dedicated" is also not truly established or "exhausted", and how all the phenomena of the dedicated, the dedicator, and the object of dedication are unproduced and "exhausted" by their own essence—meaning they do not have even the slightest establishment from their own side—and therefore, ultimately, how the dedicating phenomena or mind "never dedicates" or does not have any virtue to dedicate to "whichever phenomena" are the objects of dedication; then being embraced by "thoroughly knowing" "thus" that the three spheres of dedication are not truly existent is called "excellent thorough dedication". ^3-131

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-132]]

Regarding this, others say: If pure and impure dedication are posited by the absence or presence of the perception of signs, why do some sutras teach apprehension as primary? That was taught in order to lead some beginners, but to teach that virtues bound by manifest adherence are not entirely pure, it is stated: "If" one has "the perception of signs" regarding the three—the action, agent, and object—of dedication and makes dedication while being "presumptuous", "that is not perfectly dedicating." ^3-132

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-133]]

"If," on the other hand, one has the perception that those three phenomena lack the signs of true establishment and dedicates by way of "thoroughly knowing" them to be not truly existent and illusion-like, "that is perfectly thoroughly dedicating" to "awakening". For example, "just as" eating "good food mixed with poison" appears pleasant temporarily but ultimately ruins one, "similarly", even though "white phenomena" are virtuous, "apprehending" them with the perception of true adherence is "said by the Victor" to be like eating good food mixed with poison. ^3-133

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-134]]

Having thus taught the manner of dedication extensively, to summarize it is stated: Since the ways of pure and impure dedication are as previously explained, one should "thus" understand the faults and qualities and train in unmistaken "thorough dedication". How? With the gnosis that unmistakenly realizes and knows the rejoicing of "those" Victors, the perfectly complete "Buddhas", the virtues to be dedicated, and the manner of dedication, etc., they know whatever is the essence or aspect of dedication conventionally, whatever infallible result arises from it and what it is like, and what their nature of ultimate non-apprehension is like. Having realized their status just as it is, regarding how they rejoice and dedicate, one thinks: "I too shall rejoice in accordance with that and make such a thorough dedication." By thinking this and dedicating, even if one is unskilled in how to dedicate oneself, because one dedicates in accordance with how the Victors dedicate, it is entirely pure dedication. As it is said in the Aspiration of Good Conduct, "Just as the hero Mañjuśrī knows..." and so forth. To teach the result of dedicating "thus": If one "thoroughly dedicates" all the merit of oneself and others to great "awakening" by way of being embraced by the wisdom that realizes them as non-apprehendable "just as it was explained", it is a perfectly pure dedication, akin to "good food without poison", and its result is not abandoning or attaining Buddhahood, and one is unmistakenly speaking the Mahayana Dharma "spoken by the Victor". ^3-134

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-135]]

"That bodhisattva", "the hero" who "thoroughly dedicates" "thus" in the manner of not apprehending things as true, "outshines" "all those" "bodhisattvas in the world" who have an apprehension that grasps at signs. Thus it is said. This concludes the commentary on the sixth chapter, on thorough dedication, from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection, called the Jewel Lamp that Fulfills the Hopes of the Fortunate, Illuminating the Suchness of Profound Dependent Arising. ^3-135

###### **Path of Meditation of Practice** ^3-3-2-1-2-10-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-136]]

Path of meditation of accomplishment. Tenth, to teach the path of meditation of accomplishment, it is said in the Ornament: From "Its essence, supremacy..." up to "...directing it, is of great purpose." The definition of the path of meditation of accomplishment is: any undefiled Mahayana subsequent clear realization that establishes the lasting result of the ultimate realization which is to be attained by oneself. When divided, there are five: the essence of that path of meditation of accomplishment, the supremacy of the path of meditation of accomplishment of the perfection of wisdom, that path of meditation not constructing all phenomena as truly established, that path of meditation directing all phenomena toward lacking the apprehension of true establishment, and the great purpose of attaining Buddhahood as the result of that path of meditation. Its boundaries are from the first to the tenth ground. To teach such a path of meditation of accomplishment, the four verses beginning with "Blind without a guide..." teach what is called supremacy among the five paths of meditation of accomplishment. Furthermore, "for example", even if "a hundred million" "blind" people lacking eyes, without a "guide" or leader to lead them on the path, assemble together, if they "do not see" and do not know the "way" to go to the "city", then "how could they" enter that path and "enter the city"? "There is no way" that could happen. ^3-136

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-137]]

"Similarly", "if" one lacks "this" eye-like perfection of "wisdom" that realizes the abiding nature, "these five perfections, giving and so forth", which are like those "without eyes", are not embraced by wisdom—which becomes the essence of accomplishment that is like a guide leading to higher grounds such as the Extremely Joyful—or lack that companion, and so they "are not able to touch" the city of perfect "awakening". "When" the paths of "giving and so forth" "are perfectly embraced by" the "wisdom" that realizes the three spheres as not truly existent, "at that time" "they", giving and so forth, "attain eyes", "attain the name of these perfections", and easily cause one to go to or attain the city of omniscience. ^3-137

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-138]]

For example regarding those, even if the work of painting figures such as a king has been completely painted in all other respects, if only the eyes are absent or missing, then as long as the eyes are not painted, the painting is incomplete, so one does not receive wages or a reward. Therefore, having gained certainty in how this very wisdom that realizes emptiness endowed with all supreme aspects is the supreme essence of the Mahayana path, those of the supreme vehicle's disposition should actualize that very excellent path within their own mindstreams through hearing, contemplating, and meditating. "When", regarding "conditioned" phenomena—the three realms—and "unconditioned" phenomena—emptiness—and "white" phenomena—virtue embraced by bodhicitta—and "black" phenomena—phenomena from virtue in the mindstreams of śrāvakas and below—having examined and investigated them with the "wisdom" that discerns the abiding nature, and having "completely destroyed" the focal target of sign-grasping, one realizes that not even a mere subtle "particle" of them is established from its own side and they are "unapprehendable" and non-existent, at "that time", it "is counted as the perfection of wisdom in the worlds". For example, "space" is merely the negation of obstruction and contact, but because it has no form that acts as its own essence, it "does not abide in anything" at all; it is "like that". ^3-138

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-139]]

At that time, in order to teach that manifest adherence to the dharmadhātu as true is also a mistake, it is said: "If", even though embraced by special skillful means, being bound by sign-grasping, one "wanders" through adhering as true to the object, emptiness, thinking "I will engage in this perfection of wisdom, the mother of the Jinas," and has a mind of manifest adherence as true, thinking "I will liberate a hundred million sentient beings who touch suffering or are tormented by suffering from their suffering," then since they perceive self and other sentient beings as true, "that bodhisattva" with complete conceptualization "is called one with apprehension"; "such as this" is "not engaging in the supreme" or genuine "perfection of wisdom." In the Eight Thousand, it is said: "The perfection of wisdom is that which illuminates," and so forth. After this, although the perfectly pure path of meditation should be taught, as it is said in the Ornament: "Relying on Buddhas, giving and so forth," one needs to know the two causes of the arising and non-arising of the perfectly pure path of meditation, so first, to teach the cause of its arising, the meaning of the one verse taught beginning with "Whatever bodhisattva..." is: "Whatever bodhisattva" has "pleased the Tathāgatas" in "previous" lifetimes and, when "engaging in the conduct of a conqueror's son," engages in conduct by means of respectfully listening to, holding, and contemplating this profound sutra of the perfection of wisdom, they, "born now in this world," by the power of past latencies and excellent causes, become skilled in the meaning of the perfection of wisdom and "free from doubt" or hesitation "regarding the profound meaning," and "as soon as they hear this" sutra, that person generates the perception of the Teacher, thinking "I have seen the Teacher," and by that cause, they will "swiftly realize" the perfectly pure path of meditation, the "great peace" of awakening. Thus it is said. ^3-139

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-140]]

To teach the cause of non-arising, the two verses beginning with "When practicing previously..." are taught. The meaning is: "Whatever bodhisattva", "when" engaging in the conduct of a conqueror's son "previously", has "honored" "many hundreds of millions of Buddhas" and "accumulated merit" by offering vast requisites and so forth, but has not engaged in hearing, contemplating, and so forth regarding the perfection of wisdom, the mother of the Jinas, and lacking faith, has "not made aspirations" to realize its meaning, then even if they have attained a human body "now", upon hearing this profound sutra of the perfection of wisdom, "at that moment", "that one of small intellect" who is "unable to realize the profound meaning" "abandons" this sutra of the perfection of wisdom and does not hold it; and because by abandoning it they have also abandoned the jewel of the Buddha, and become devoid of the refuge of the holy Dharma and the companionship of the noble Sangha, "they go to the Avīci hell" and will "experience suffering for an eon" and so forth; thus limitless disadvantages are taught. Thus it is said. ^3-140

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-141]]

Having summarized the meaning of arising and non-arising, the instruction with an example that it is proper to train in the sutra of the perfection of wisdom with faith and respect is: Because the benefits and disadvantages of having or not having faith in the perfection of wisdom are like that, "if" one desires to "touch" or attain the supreme "exalted wisdom of a Buddha," one "should generate faith in this mother of the Jinas" and engage in it through hearing, contemplating, and meditating; thus the disciples of future generations are instructed out of compassion. The reason why this is necessary is that, "now", having met the Buddha's teachings which are like an "island of jewels", and in this time of having the protection of spiritual friends, one must make it meaningful: "for example, if merchants go to an island of jewels", it is improper if they deplete their previous merchandise and, without finding any jewels at all, "return empty-handed again", because there is no greater loss or worse situation than that. Therefore, fortunate ones should rely on joyous effort to accomplish the two goals upon a support of leisure, like the continuous flow of a river. This is the commentary on the seventh chapter, the Chapter on Hell, from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection titled The Jewel Lamp that Fulfills the Hopes of the Fortunate and Illuminates the Suchness of Profound Dependent Arising. ^3-141

###### **Path of Meditation of Complete Purity** ^3-3-2-1-2-11-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-142]]

The perfectly pure path of meditation. Eleventh, the perfectly pure path of meditation: In the Ornament, it is said: "The purity of the result is form and so forth..." up to "...and so forth is the pure path." The defining characteristic of the perfectly pure path of meditation is: whatever uncontaminated Mahayana subsequent clear realization establishes the imprint of the ultimate abandonment that is to be attained by oneself. When divided, there are nine perfectly pure paths of meditation, which are the antidotes to the nine cycles of objects abandoned by meditation, ranging from the small of the small to the great of the great. The meditative path of achievement and this perfectly pure path of meditation are the same in meaning, merely being conceptually distinct. Its boundaries range from the first ground to the tenth ground. Regarding such a perfectly pure path of meditation, from among two sections: ^3-142

###### **General Explanation** ^3-3-2-1-2-11-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-143]]

General teaching. First, the general teaching: It is the one verse line beginning with "The purity of form" and so forth. The meaning is: Furthermore, because stains do not enter into the abiding nature, all phenomena subsumed by the aggregates, elements, and sense sources, such as form and so forth, are primordially perfectly pure and naturally luminous; however, because they are apprehended mistakenly by the ignorance of true-grasping, afflictions such as attachment arise, so they are called adventitious stains. As long as one does not clearly see the abiding nature because it is obscured by true-grasping, form and so forth are said to be perfectly impure. When the obscuring stains are purified and one clearly sees the abiding nature, because of the vital point that the fact that the abiding nature of form and so forth is primordially perfectly pure without being corrupted by stains matches one's own mode of seeing, the object—the purity of the aggregate of form and so forth from stains—and the subject—the purity of the result, ranging from the path of release of the path of seeing, which is the perfection of wisdom or the result of asceticism, up to Buddhahood—have become one in meaning. Therefore, since the purity of the result cannot be posited separately from the "purity of form" and so forth, one should know that those two cannot be conceptually divided and are one in essence; and in dependence on that mode of the purity of the result, at that time it is said that the object, form, is pure. For example, when falling hairs appear upon a form, at that time it is said that the form is not clear, but when the cause of delusion, visual aberration and so forth, is eliminated, it is said that the form is clear; it is like that. ^3-143

###### **Specific Explanation of Complete Purity** ^3-3-2-1-2-11-2-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-144]]

Explaining the specific perfect purities. Second, regarding explaining the specific perfect purities, three verse lines beginning with "the purity of the result, form" and so forth are spoken. Furthermore, as is said in the Ornament: "Because of being weakened by the three paths concerning afflictions and objects of knowledge, it is the purity of disciples, rhinoceros-like ones, and sons of the Jinas; for a Buddha, it is thoroughly [pure] in all aspects." Accordingly, because Śrāvaka Arhats have abandoned merely the afflictive obscurations, and rhinoceros-like Pratyekabuddha Arhats have abandoned the afflictive obscurations and one part of the cognitive obscurations—merely the conceptuality of the apprehended—their perfect purity is partial; it is not thoroughly pure. Because the cessations and paths in the mind-stream of a noble Buddha have abandoned the two obscurations without remainder, it is thoroughly perfect purity or ultimate perfect purity. Just as it was explained previously that "the purity of form" and "the purity of the result" are one in essence, similarly, when the stains of the objects to be abandoned allocated to the uninterrupted path that acts as the attainer of the path of release, which is the result, are purified, its object, form and so forth, must also be posited as being purified by those very stains in one's mind-stream. The subject isolated from the single object of purification—which is the perfection of wisdom or the perfectly pure result—and the perfectly pure object, form and so forth, are indivisible in essence and cannot be conceptually divided as different. Likewise, when the nine cycles of the Mahayana path of meditation from the second ground up to the tenth ground act as the direct antidotes to their respective allocated objects to be abandoned—the conceptuality of the apprehended and the apprehender abandoned by meditation, from the great up to the small of the small—and abandon the two obscurations without remainder, it becomes the thoroughly perfectly pure result subsumed by the cessations and paths in the mind-stream of a Buddha, the knowledge of all aspects. Thus, these three purities—the ultimate purity which is the purity of the knowledge of all aspects, the purity of attaining the cessations and paths which are the respective results of the path of training, and the purity of the phenomena of the bases (the aggregates, elements, and sense sources such as form and so forth)—merely appear as different conventionally; ultimately, just as the element of space has no divisions into different essences, the naturally perfectly pure dharmadhatu is indivisible into different essences, and cannot even be conceptually separated as different, because all dharmadhatus are of a single nature. Thus, having finished teaching the eleven phenomena that characterize the knowledge of the path, the summary of the chapter is: This is the commentary on the second chapter from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection titled The Jewel Lamp that Fulfills the Hopes of the Fortunate and Illuminates the Suchness of Profound Dependent Arising. ^3-144

###### Knowledge of the Bases ^3-3-2-1-3-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-145]]

Knowledge of the bases. Thus, having finished explaining the cause, the knowledge of the path, regarding teaching the knowledge of the bases which is that which perfectly purifies the knowledge of the path: it is said in the Ornament of Clear Realization: "Through knowledge one does not abide in existence..." up to "...is asserted as the knowledge of all those [bases]." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the bases is: an exalted wisdom in the mind-stream of a person who possesses it, which is embraced by the wisdom that directly realizes selflessness, and which abides in the class of realization of the Hinayana. Regarding the phenomena that characterize the knowledge of the bases, there are nine: the knowledge of the path of not abiding in existence through knowledge; the knowledge of the path of not abiding in peace through compassion; the knowledge of the bases that is distant from the Resultant Mother; the knowledge of the bases that is close to the Resultant Mother; the knowledge of the bases of the discordant class; the preparation of the knowledge of the bases; the equality of the mode of apprehension of the preparation; and the path of seeing, since there are nine. To teach the first two, there is one verse beginning with "Not on the near shore, the far shore, or the limits..." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the path of not abiding in existence through knowledge is: a noble Mahayana being's knowledge that abides in the class of realization that stops the extreme of existence in relation to the conventional bases. That and the knowledge of the path that knows the path of the Śrāvakas are one in meaning. If divided, there are three: the Mahayana path of seeing, path of meditation, and path of no more learning. Its boundaries are from the Mahayana path of seeing up to the ground of Buddhahood. The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the path of not abiding in peace through compassion is: a knowledge in a noble being's mind-stream that abides in the class of realization that stops the extreme of peace in relation to the conventional bases. That and a noble Mahayana being's knowledge that abides in the class of realization of exceptional methods are one in meaning. If divided, there are three paths of seeing, meditation, and no more learning of the Mahayana that become it. Its boundaries are like the previous one. ^3-145

###### **Path-Knowledge that Does Not Abide in Existence through Wisdom and Path-Knowledge that Does Not Abide in Peace through Compassion** ^3-3-2-1-3-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-146]]

The knowledge of the path of not abiding in existence through knowledge and the knowledge of the path of not abiding in peace through compassion. To explain those two: not abiding in existence through such knowledge and the knowledge of the path of abiding in peace through compassion, the meaning of the one verse spoken beginning with "The heroes, wherever they act" and so forth is: The heroes, the bodhisattvas, who cannot be swayed by true-grasping and self-cherishing, wherever they act in the domains of samsara, see the faults of samsara; and by abiding in the perfection of wisdom embraced by exceptional means and knowledge, they perfectly pass beyond birth in samsara caused by karma and afflictions without being attached to any domain of samsara in the three realms; and even though they have passed beyond, they do not abide in the one-sided peace of nirvana that is merely liberation from existence like the Śrāvakas, so they do not abide in either of the extremes of existence or peace. Even though they have cleared away the karma and afflictions of powerless birth in existence, by the power of compassion and aspiration they thoroughly demonstrate taking birth in samsara. As it is said in the Sublime Continuum: "Because they are noble, they have eradicated the suffering of sickness, aging, and death." Like that, although they do not have aging, sickness, and death, for the sake of others they demonstrate dying, transmigrating, and so forth, because they are experts who possess exceptional means, such as great compassion and bodhicitta, and exceptional wisdom that realizes existence and peace as empty of true existence. This directly teaches the two knowledges of the path of not abiding in the two extremes of existence and peace, and implicitly teaches the two: the knowledge of the bases that is distant from the Resultant Mother due to not being expert in the means of stopping the two extremes of existence and peace, and the knowledge of the bases that is close to the Resultant Mother due to being expert in those means. Also from the Eight Thousand Stanza: "Bhagavan, the perfection of wisdom does not abide on the near shore, it does not abide on the far shore, nor does it abide afar in between the two," and so forth is spoken. ^3-146

###### **Knowledge of the Bases Distant from the Resultant Mother** ^3-3-2-1-3-3-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-147]]

The knowledge of the bases that is distant from the Resultant Mother. Third, regarding teaching the knowledge of the bases that is distant from the Resultant Mother, it is said in the Ornament: "Through the door of apprehending marks, it is distant because of lacking skill in means." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the bases that is distant from the Resultant Mother is: a knowledge of the bases that is separated from great compassion and bound by true-grasping. That and the knowledge of the bases of the discordant class are one in meaning. Its boundaries are from the Hinayana path of seeing up to the Hinayana path of learning. ^3-147

###### **Knowledge of the Bases Close to the Resultant Mother** ^3-3-2-1-3-4-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-148]]

The knowledge of the bases that is close to the Resultant Mother. Fourth, regarding the knowledge of the bases that is close to the Resultant Mother, it is said in the Ornament: "Because of being expert in means, it is explained as being perfectly close." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the bases that is close to the Resultant Mother is: a knowledge in the mind-stream of a noble Mahayana being that abides in the class of realization of the Hinayana, which is embraced by great compassion and the wisdom that directly realizes emptiness. That and the knowledge of the bases of the antidote class are one in meaning. Its boundaries are from the Mahayana path of seeing up to the ground of Buddhahood. The manner in which these two are taught by this sutra is just as explained above. ^3-148

###### **Knowledge of the Bases of the Discordant Class** ^3-3-2-1-3-5-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-149]]

The knowledge of the bases of the discordant class. Fifth, regarding the knowledge of the bases of the discordant class, it is said in the Ornament: "The emptiness of the aggregates of form and so forth..." up to "...the discordant class." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the bases of the discordant class is: a knowledge of the bases that is separated from the two exceptional means and wisdom. That and the knowledge of the bases bound by true-grasping in the mind-stream of a noble Hinayana being are one in meaning. Its boundaries are from the Hinayana path of seeing up to the Hinayana path of no more learning. To teach that very thing, the meaning of the three lines spoken in this sutra beginning with "These beings..." is: These beings who are obscured by ignorance, having become attached through grasping as true to the mud of the five aggregates of name and form which are difficult to cross, wander in the wheel of samsara from the peak of existence down to the Unrelenting Hell, like the continuously turning wheel of the spherical wind that propels the constellations; because the twelve links of dependent origination from ignorance to aging occur in a continuous sequence like a firebrand wheel. Knowing that these deluded beings caught in this state of samsara are tormented by many sufferings and are difficult to liberate, similar to deer caught in an inescapable trap, the bodhisattvas who are expert in wisdom and means should act for the sake of others without being attached to the state of samsara. ^3-149

###### **Knowledge of the Bases of the Antidotal Class** ^3-3-2-1-3-6-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-150]]

The knowledge of the bases of the antidote class. Sixth, regarding the knowledge of the bases of the antidote class that is embraced by the antidote to apprehending marks, it is said in the Ornament: "There is no grasping at an 'I' in generosity and so forth..." up to "...that is asserted to be inconceivable." The defining characteristic of the knowledge of the bases of the antidote class is: a knowledge in the mind-stream of a noble Mahayana being that abides in the class of realization of the Hinayana, which is embraced by the two exceptional means and wisdom. That and the knowledge of the bases in the mind-stream of a noble Mahayana being are one in meaning. Its boundaries are from the Mahayana path of seeing up to the ground of Buddhahood. To teach that very thing, it is said: "Those endowed with wisdom roam like a bird in the sky." The meaning is: having known the way that obscured beings are suffering and difficult to liberate in this state of samsara, like deer caught in a trap, the bodhisattvas who are endowed with the non-apprehending affection of great compassion and the wisdom that realizes the abiding nature, and who are not bound by the delusion of grasping the aggregates and so forth as true, roam for the sake of others without abiding with clinging attachment in this state of samsara, just as a bird roams in the sky yet the bird does not abide in the sky. ^3-150

###### **Application of the Knowledge of the Bases** ^3-3-2-1-3-7-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-151]]

The training of the knowledge of the bases. Seventh, regarding teaching the training of the knowledge of the bases, it is said in the Ornament: "Form and so forth, its impermanence and so forth..." up to "...are made known by the sevenfold appearances." The defining characteristic of the bodhisattva training taught in the context of the knowledge of the bases is: a yoga of a bodhisattva meditated as an antidote to either the mistaken adherence to the essence and attributes of the conventional mode of abiding of the basis, or the mistaken adherence to the essence and attributes of the ultimate mode of abiding. When divided, there are ten: the training that refutes true-grasping to the basis of attributes, form and so forth; the training that refutes true-grasping to the attributes, impermanence and so forth; the training of form and so forth being unperfected and perfected as a basis of good qualities; and the training of non-attachment through true-grasping to form and so forth—these four by way of the object; the training in the perfection of wisdom not increasing or decreasing ultimately; the training of the agent lacking true existence; the training of the three difficult deeds; the training of fruits existing conventionally even though they do not exist ultimately; the training of awakening not depending on others; and the training that refutes true-grasping to the sevenfold appearances by way of illustrating examples. Its boundaries are from the Mahayana path of accumulation up to the end of the continuum. ^3-151

###### **Equality of the Mode of Apprehension of that Application** ^3-3-2-1-3-8-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-152]]

The equality of the mode of apprehension of that training. Eighth, regarding teaching the equality of the mode of apprehension of that training, it is said: "The absence of conceit toward form and so forth... its equality is fourfold." The defining characteristic of the equality of the bodhisattva training taught in the context of the knowledge of the bases is: a yoga of a bodhisattva embraced by the wisdom that refutes true-grasping to the divisions of the bases, definition and definiendum, and object and object-possessor. When divided, there are four: the equality of the training in the absence of conceit of true existence regarding the essence of form and so forth; the equality of the training in the absence of conceit of true existence regarding its definition and definiendum; the equality of the training in the absence of conceit of true existence regarding its divisions; and the equality of the training in the absence of conceit of true existence regarding its object and object-possessor. Its boundaries are similar to the training. To explain these two in summary, one verse is spoken beginning with "Thoroughly pure conduct..." The meaning is: Whichever bodhisattva realizes all phenomena of the aggregates and so forth as the equality of emptiness of true existence and whose conduct is thoroughly pure, does not engage through evident true-grasping to the essence of form and its attributes of impermanence and so forth. Likewise, if they do not engage through true-grasping to consciousness, discrimination, the three feelings of pleasure, pain, and neutrality, and the mental factor of intention, then this conduct of a child of the Victorious One, which does not engage with true-grasping to any object whatsoever in that way, thoroughly abandons all attachment of true-grasping; and they engage in the perfection of wisdom of the Sugatas, which is entirely liberated from all attachment of true-grasping—so it is said. This is the commentary on the eighth chapter, "Total Purity," from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection, called "The Jewelled Lamp that Illuminates the Suchness of Profound Dependent Origination, Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate." ^3-152

###### **Path of Seeing** ^3-3-2-1-3-9-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-153]]

The path of seeing. Ninth, regarding teaching the path of seeing, it occurs from: "The truth of suffering and so forth..." up to "...the moments of complete knowledge." The defining characteristic of the path of seeing explicitly taught in this context is: a clear realization of the Mahayana truth that directly realizes freedom from conceptual proliferation explicitly taught in this context. When divided, there are sixteen moments of Mahayana knowledge and forbearance explicitly taught in this context. Its boundaries are exclusively the Mahayana path of seeing. To explain this very path of seeing, one verse is spoken beginning with "Conducting themselves thus..." The meaning is: Just as explained before, whichever bodhisattva realizes all phenomena as the freedom from conceptual proliferation of the emptiness of true existence and familiarizes with it, thus gradually training and engaging on the path, being skilled in the meaning of the natural state and having clear intelligence, attains the Mahayana path of seeing, severs all attachment of true-grasping, and becomes liberated from all objects to be abandoned by seeing without exception; and they proceed higher and higher to the path of meditation and so forth without the attachment of evident grasping. ^3-153

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-154]]

For example, just as the sun remains brightly shining when free from the planetary eclipse called Rāhu, and just as a fire burns all grass, trees, and forests when kindled, the non-conceptual exalted wisdom of method and wisdom united in tandem, like the light of the sun and fire, clears away and subdues all the darkness and forests of the two obscurations—so it is said. According to the master, one must make a chapter break here, for he applied this single verse beginning with "All phenomena..." to the brief teaching of the training in complete aspects. According to the commentary on the Verse Summary by Jñānapāda, the section downwards from "To the Victorious Ones, the victory of the gods..." is applied to the training in complete aspects, and a chapter break is made at the beginning of that, and it appears that most manuscripts of the Verse Summary do likewise. Nevertheless, our own system follows the master's commentary; as Gungtang Jamyang also makes the chapter conclusion in his annotated commentary and so forth, the chapter conclusion after having finished explaining the nine phenomena that characterize the knowledge of the bases is: This is the commentary on the third chapter from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection, called "The Jewelled Lamp that Illuminates the Suchness of Profound Dependent Origination, Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate." ^3-154

##### Four Applications of Practice ^3-3-2-2-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-155]]

The four trainings of practice. Having finished explaining the three knowledges as objects to be known in that way, the second is the explanation of the presentation of the four trainings of practice. From among the cause and effect of mastering the trainings and the cause and effect of stabilizing them: ^3-155

###### Result of Mastery over the Applications ^3-3-2-2-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-156]]

The cause and effect of mastering the trainings. Regarding the first, there are two: the training in complete aspects which is the cause of mastery, and the culminating training which is the fruit. ^3-156

###### **Application of the Complete Aspects, the Cause of Mastery** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-157]]

The training in complete aspects which is the cause of mastery. Regarding teaching the first, it is said in the Ornament: "The aspects together with the trainings..." up to "...this is together with skillful means." The defining characteristic of the training in complete aspects is: a yoga of a bodhisattva embraced by the wisdom that meditates having summarized the aspects of the three knowledges. That and the knowledge of a bodhisattva are synonymous. When divided, there are one hundred and seventy-three by way of aspects, and twenty by way of trainings. Its boundaries are from the Mahayana path of accumulation up to the end of the continuum. There are eleven phenomena that characterize the training in complete aspects, because there are eleven: the aspects, the trainings, the good qualities, the faults of training, the defining characteristics, the aids to liberation, the sangha of irreversible learners, the training in the equality of existence and peace, the training in pure realms, and the training in skillful means. ^3-157

###### **Aspects** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-158]]

The aspects. Regarding teaching the first, it is said in the Ornament: "The particulars of the knowledge of the bases..." up to "...are asserted as thirty-nine." Regarding the aspects taught here, from among the two, the defining characteristic and divisions, the first is: a knowledge capable of overcoming its own incompatible class is the defining characteristic of the aspect of knowledge of the class of antidotes taught in this context. Second, when divided, there are twenty-seven aspects of knowledge of the knowledge of the bases, thirty-six aspects of knowledge of the knowledge of the paths, and one hundred and ten aspects of knowledge of the omniscient knowledge, making one hundred and seventy-three. Regarding establishing the nature of the aspects that are the objects of meditation as such, the meaning taught in one verse beginning with "All phenomena..." is: When a bodhisattva looks at and abides in the supreme meaning of the perfection of wisdom that is the nature of the training in complete aspects that realizes suchness, the natural state—all phenomena from form up to omniscient knowledge being pure of true establishment or natural establishment, and completely pure from having entered into being tainted by stains in the natural state—the bodhisattva who is the meditator of the perfection of wisdom is not observed as truly existent, and all phenomena of the bases, paths, and aspects to be looked at are also not observed as truly existent; thus this one-pointed abiding in the expanse of the freedom from conceptual proliferation of the emptiness of true existence is engaging in the supreme perfection of wisdom that is the nature of the bodhisattva's path of clear realization—so it is said. This teaches by way of what embraces the one hundred and seventy-three aspects. One should know that Buddhaśrī applying this verse to the path of seeing of the third chapter in his commentary on the Verse Summary is in disagreement with the master's commentary. This is the commentary on the ninth chapter, the chapter of praise, from the Commentary on the Verses of the Precious Qualities Collection, called "The Jewelled Lamp that Illuminates the Suchness of Profound Dependent Origination, Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate." ^3-158

###### **Application** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-2-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-159]]

The trainings. Regarding the second, the explanation of the trainings that meditate on those aspects: Since identifying the person who is the support of the meditator of the training must precede this, regarding teaching first the vessel for the training, four verses are spoken starting with "To the Jina..." The meaning is: In response to the question asked to the Jina, the Buddha, the Bhagavan, by Śakra, the king of gods, "How do bodhisattvas who have done the surpassing deeds toward former Jinas, created roots of virtue in that regard, and possess the protection of spiritual friends, exert themselves in the conduct of the perfection of wisdom and meditate through the training?" he replied: Realizing that not even an atom of true establishment is observed in phenomena such as the five aggregates and eighteen elements, they do not exert themselves with adherence to true existence, and abiding in the training of non-observation toward phenomena such as the aggregates, they do not exert themselves with manifest adherence—that is the unmistaken exertion of bodhisattvas. Whichever person, having heard the explanation from the sutra of the Mother that although all these phenomena from form up to omniscient knowledge are not naturally established, all activities of cause and effect and dependent origination are feasible in mere convention, like an emanation and a magical illusion, that sentient being who not only is not terrified of its meaning but also is without doubt, and earnestly practices and trains with continuous faith and joy in learning the perfection of wisdom, should be known as having entered the path of the Mahayana since a long time ago. It should be known that they have done the surpassing deeds of venerating, making offerings, and offering temples and so forth to many tens of millions of billions of former Buddhas, because without special causes preceding, it would not occur that one finds convinced faith in the profound meaning of emptiness and practices it. Regarding teaching by way of a verse example of relief in order to realize the activities of having passed beyond obstacles such as to hearing the Mahayana: For example, when a person who has entered a vast path of many yojanas through an unpopulated wilderness where there are great fears of bandits and so forth, traveling on that path, at some point sees the excellent, pleasing forests at the boundary between the wilderness and the populated areas where the cowherds of the city dwell, those people think, "These are signs of being close to villages and cities," and find relief from the fears of bandits, wild animals, and so forth, and from then on will be without the fears of bandits and so forth. Similarly, when one who desires to attain holy awakening for the sake of others and completely seeks the path of means to attain it, whoever attains the good fortune of hearing this Mother of the Jinas, the perfection of wisdom, and engaging in it through hearing, thinking, and meditating, attains relief from the suffering of saṃsāra and has no fear of falling into the extremes of existence and peace; they will not fall to the level of a śrāvaka arhat, nor will they fall to the level of a pratyekabuddha who realizes conditions, but are irreversible from the path of the Mahayana—so it is said. ^3-159

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-160]]

Having thus taught the support, regarding the entity of the training that is supported: It is stated in the Ornament, from "Because of not abiding in form and so forth..." up to "...that is pure, along with the boundaries." Regarding the training, there are three: the definition, the divisions, and the boundaries. The first: The yoga of a bodhisattva embraced by the wisdom of the union of calm abiding and special insight observing emptiness that is explicitly taught in this context, is the definition of the principal training explicitly taught in this context. Second, if divided: the training of not abiding with adherence to true existence in form and so forth, the training of refuting abiding in those with true adherence, the profound training of realizing the suchness of the four of form and so forth, the hard-to-fathom training of realizing the nature of the path, and the immeasurable training of realizing the nature of the aspects of form and so forth to be immeasurable—these five are the division by way of entity; the training of attaining prophecy, the stable training of irreversibility, the training of definite emergence, the uninterrupted training, the training of being close to awakening, the training of swiftly becoming a Buddha, and the training for the sake of others—these seven are the division by way of the person who is the support; the training of realizing that ultimately there is no increase and no decrease, the training of not seeing phenomena and non-phenomena and so forth ultimately, the training of not seeing form and so forth [which are] inconceivable, and the training of not truly conceptualizing form and so forth as characteristic and characterized and the non-conceptual training—these four are the division by way of the method of completing the meditation on the Mother; the training that grants the precious result, and the completely pure training—these two are the division by way of the result; and the training of the boundaries, [making] nineteen. The training that is difficult to realize over a long time with great hardship is not an actual principal training but merely a training, so when counted, there are twenty trainings. Third, the boundaries: The difficult-to-realize training of great hardship [exists] from the Mahayana path of accumulation, and the principal trainings exist from the heat stage of the Mahayana path of preparation up to the end of the continuum. Since there is no way to directly correlate the other previous trainings with the sutra, regarding teaching the entity of the uninterrupted training by way of a preceding example, the meaning of the two verses spoken starting with "A person... the ocean's" is: For example, when a person goes in order to see the water of the ocean, if that person sees trees, forests, and mountains in front of them, they know that the great ocean is still far away, whereas if they do not see those signs of distance such as mountains, but all the land becomes gradually even and low, and they cannot see the far edge, they think, "Now the great ocean has become close to me," and are without doubt about it and know it even from afar. ^3-160

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-161]]

Like that example, those who have thoroughly entered into supreme awakening for the sake of others and have obtained the fortune to gradually engage through hearing, thinking, and meditating on this Mother of the Victors, the perfection of wisdom, should also be known as being close to the great ocean of the dharmakaya, the exalted wisdom of omniscient knowledge, because they have attained the special capacity to accomplish awakening. Therefore, even if that person possessing the continuum of the principal training of the Mahayana path is directly prophesied by the Guides saying, "You will awaken," they know, thinking, "Before long, I will touch or attain the awakening of a Buddha," and when the conceptions to be abandoned by the path of seeing, which are like the signs of mountains and forests, do not appear on the [level of] supreme dharma, they know they are close to the path of seeing, which is like the ocean; this also teaches the quality of attaining the dharmakaya. Following that, in order to teach the tenth training, the training of being close to awakening—which has passed beyond the conceptions of the path of preparation below [the level of] supreme dharma, which are like old leaves, and has become the support for new uncontaminated dharmas, which are like fresh leaves—one verse is spoken, starting with "In the good time of spring..." For example, when in the good time of spring withered leaves from a tree trunk have fallen to the ground, just as that tree's branches will before long newly produce or generate leaves, fruits, and flowers, similarly, whatever person obtains this perfection of wisdom in their hands will before long attain the awakening of the Guides, and with a form body like a wish-fulfilling tree, they will fulfill the hopes of wandering beings according to their wishes. This also teaches the quality of accomplishing the sambhogakaya. ^3-161

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-162]]

In order to teach the training of swiftly attaining perfect awakening, the meaning of the one verse spoken starting with "For example, a woman..." is: For example, when any pregnant woman with a child in her womb becomes afflicted by experiencing the pain of giving birth, that situation is called the measure of the time having arrived for her to give birth to the child; similarly, whatever bodhisattva, through hearing and thinking about this sutra of the perfection of wisdom of the Victors, generates the joy of a thoroughly clear mind and the seeking aspiration desiring to attain the higher paths, and puts them into practice through training, will swiftly touch great awakening. This is just as the Lord of Reasoning stated: "The inference that an effect will arise from an assembled collection of causes, because it does not depend on anything else, is expressed as being [an inference of] nature." This also teaches the quality of attaining the nirmanakaya. Although Buddhaśrī explained this differently, it is arranged [here] according to the Master's commentary. Regarding the training that refutes increase and decrease, the meaning taught by the two lines starting with "The perfection of wisdom..." is: When properly engaging in hearing, thinking, and meditating on the meaning of the supreme perfection of wisdom, that yogi meditating on the training does not ultimately see the increase—that is, the production and the distinguished states illustrated by that—and the complete decrease—that is, the cessation and the deteriorated states—of the phenomena of the thoroughly afflicted and completely purified sides, such as form and so forth, because all phenomena are ultimately without increase and decrease, being empty of true existence and like an illusion. ^3-162

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-163]]

To explain the training of not seeing dharma and non-dharma and so forth, the meaning of the occurrence of the two lines starting with "Whatever dharma..." is: "Even if whatever bodhisattva does not ultimately see virtuous dharmas, non-virtuous non-dharmas, and the way dharmas abide, suchness, the dharmadhatu, and abides in suchness free from elaborations in which all referents of apprehending signs are thoroughly pacified, they do not touch—that is, they do not abide in—the nirvana of mere one-sided peace, and that awakening is an abiding in supreme wisdom that has passed beyond the two extremes of existence and peace." ^3-163

###### **Qualities of the Application** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-3-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-164]]

The qualities of the training. Third, regarding the qualities of the training, the Ornament states: "Crushing the power of maras and so forth, / Are the fourteen aspects of qualities." The definition of a quality of a Mahayana training is: the benefit obtained by the power of meditating on a Mahayana training that serves as the method to obtain it. When divided, there are fourteen: the quality of crushing the power of maras by the power of meditating on the training, the quality of the Buddhas considering and knowing one, the quality of the Buddhas actualising one, the quality of becoming close to perfect awakening, the quality of great benefit and so forth, the quality of experiencing the object, the quality of completing all uncontaminated qualities, the quality of becoming an eloquent being, the quality of not being divided by discordant sides, the quality of generating uncommon roots of virtue, the quality of accomplishing the meaning of what was promised, the quality of thoroughly holding a vast result, the quality of accomplishing the benefit of sentient beings, and the quality of definitely attaining the Mother. The boundary of its levels is from the Mahayana path of accumulation up to the level of a Buddha. In order to generally teach those qualities, the meaning of the verse spoken starting with "Whoever engages here..." is: Whatever bodhisattva meditating on the two selflessnesses properly practices and engages here in this Mahayana training does not conceptualise by adhering as truly existent to such and such Buddha dharmas, such as the ten powers, the four fearlessnesses, and so forth; similarly, they do not conceptually apprehend as truly existent the path, the five faculties, powers, bases of miraculous power, close placements of mindfulness, seven branches of awakening, and so forth, and the result, nirvana, supreme peaceful awakening, nor do they conceptually apprehend the dharmas of the aggregates, elements, and sense sources, but rather, engaging in this Mahayana training that generates qualities such as crushing the power of maras through the blessings of the Buddhas in suchness free from elaborations, which is devoid of all conceptions of dualistic appearance, is engaging in the non-conceptual exalted wisdom of the supreme perfection of wisdom that has become the essence of all aspects. The Eight Thousand Line [Perfection of Wisdom] states: "The venerable Subhūti said this to the Bhagavān: 'Bhagavān, holding the perfection of wisdom...' and so forth." This is the commentary on the tenth chapter, Thoroughly Expressing the Qualities of Holding, from the Commentary on the Verse Summary of the Precious Qualities: The Jewel Lamp Fulfilling the Hopes of the Fortunate, Illuminating the Suchness of Profound Dependent Arising. ^3-164

###### **Faults of the Application** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-4-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-165]]

The faults of the training. Fourth, to teach the faults of the training, the Ornament states: "The faults, forty-six in number, / Should be understood with certainty." The definition of a fault of the training is: that demonic action which causes an obstacle to either the generation, the abiding, or the completion of the training. When divided, there are forty-six, from "obtaining with great difficulty" up to "generating joy not in accordance with the object". This is because there are twenty-three discordant conditions for generating the ungenerated training and twenty-three incomplete concordant conditions. The boundary of its levels is from before entering the path up to the seventh level. Since they are taught extensively in the Eight Thousand Line and so forth, there are correlations with the sutra for each one, but in this Summary, they are correlated with only about seven or eight. In this there are also two parts: explaining the faults to be eliminated, and explaining the antidotes that eliminate them. ^3-165

###### **Faults to be Eliminated** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-4-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-166]]

The faults to be eliminated. Regarding the first, from the two parts of the question and the answer: ^3-166

###### **Question** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-4-1-1-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-167]]

The question. First, regarding the question, the two lines starting with "To the Buddha, the moon among speakers..." are spoken. When the elder Subhūti asked a question of that Bhagavān Buddha, the moon among speakers who is skilled in teaching the dharma, asking: "What are the obstacles to practicing the training of the bodhisattvas who delight in the qualities of the Mahayana?" ^3-167

###### **Answer** ^3-3-2-2-1-1-4-1-2-0

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-168]]

The answer. Second, regarding the answer, the two lines starting with "The Teacher spoke..." are spoken. In answer to the question asked in that way, the Teacher spoke: "The obstacles to the training are many; out of them, here for a while just a few will be thoroughly expressed." Having provided this transition: ^3-168

![[bo-khenrab-jamyang-norbu-dronme#^3-169]]

Moreover, out of the forty-six faults of the training, there are eight actually taught here. The first of these is the instability of being proud of overly swift eloquence and turning back midway. The meaning of the occurrence of one verse starting with "By the Victor..." is: When one writes, holds, reads, explains, and listens to this perfection of wisdom, the Mother of the Victors of the three times, various uncertain and different instances of eloquence conceptualising objects such as forms and sounds, and many aspects such as excitement, dullness, sleep, regret, attachment, and hostility will arise; and due to this, without having accomplished one's own benefit of realizing the meaning of the perfection of wisdom and so forth and the benefit of other beings, activities like writing, reading, explaining, and listening will swiftly vanish like a flash of lightning, interrupting one's effort, so that one thoroughly falls away from the training; this should be known as the action of a mara. To teach apprehending the reason for turning away from the Mahayana pitaka and abandoning it, that is, the actual cause of abandoning, one verse starting with "When this is explained..." is spoken.
